However, it will not protect against a single device failure or device maintenance at AWS where your LAG is terminating.

By default, the IKE session is stopped, the tunnel goes down, and the routes are removed. You can specify that AWS must restart the IKE session when DPD timeout occurs, or you can specify that AWS must take no action when DPD timeout occurs.

== site-to-site-vpn-faq-vgw-aggregate-throughput | limit | hard=True | rel=core | vol=frequently-changing | ch=- | b=4.4.S7 | note=FAQ question: 'Is there an aggregated throughput limit for Virtual Private Gateway?' (item 17). Absorbed site-to-site-vp | st=mech-pass rev=1

Multiple VPN connections to the same Virtual Private Gateway are bound by an aggregate throughput limit from AWS to on-premises of up to 1.25 Gbps. For AWS Direct Connect connection on a Virtual Private Gateway, the throughput is bound by the Direct Connect physical port itself.

== site-to-site-vpn-faq-vgw-asn-private-only-reason | behavior | hard=False | rel=reference | vol=stable | ch=- | b=1.2.S4 | note=FAQ question: 'Why can’t I assign a public ASN for the Amazon half of the BGP session?' (item 99). | st=mech-pass rev=2
  C: Amazon does not validate ownership of ASNs, so the Amazon-side ASN is limited to private ASNs, to protect customers from BGP spoofing.
  Q: Why can you not assign a public ASN for the Amazon half of the BGP session?
  D[reason] (5): protect customers from BGP spoofing
  S: Amazon side ASN of a virtual private gateway | dom= | adj=False | cond=
  E: www/vpn/faqs :: Amazon is not validating ownership of the ASNs, therefore, we’re limiting the Amazon-side ASN to private ASNs. We want to protect customers from BGP spoofing.

== site-to-site-vpn-ipsec-aes-sha-dh-pfs | capability | hard=True | rel=core | vol=stable | ch=- | b=1.2.S4 | note=d12-003 | st=mech-pass rev=1
  C: Traffic in each tunnel can be encrypted with AES128 or AES256 and uses Diffie-Hellman groups for key exchange, which provides Perfect Forward Secrecy. AWS authenticates with SHA1 or SHA2 hashing functions.
  Q: Which encryption and hashing functions protect traffic in a Site-to-Site VPN tunnel?
  D[aes] (5): encrypted with AES128 or AES256
  D[sha] (5): SHA1 or SHA2 hashing functions
  D[pfs] (3): Perfect Forward Secrecy
  S: each Site-to-Site VPN tunnel | dom= | adj=False | cond=
  E: docs/vpn/latest/s2svpn/data-protection :: Each Site-to-Site VPN connection consists of two encrypted IPsec VPN tunnels that link AWS and your network. Traffic in each tunnel can be encrypted with AES128 or AES256 and use Diffie-Hellman groups for key exchange, providing Perfect Forward Secrecy. AWS authenticates with SHA1 or SHA2 hashing functions.
